# 7b-1 Fields Core Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add fields as a first-class value: the `Field` expression tree and its `.field` socket, the sampler and the cached kernel distances behind it, four sources, six maths nodes, the two nodes that use a field (Sample Field and Cull by Field), and the Fields library section, all exact, deterministic and never saved.

**Architecture:** `Field` is an immutable `Sendable` indirect enum in `CreatorGraph` (beside `DataTree`); `Scalar.field` and `SocketType.field` carry it, and a number or integer converts into a constant field and nothing converts back. `FieldSampler` (in `CreatorNodes`, next to the seeded noise it needs) evaluates an expression over a whole batch of points in structure-of-arrays form, checking for cancellation every 1,024 points; kernel distances come from the new `Kernel.distances(from:to:signed:)` (`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` plus a solid classifier on OCCT, exact boxes, planes and lines on the Fake) through a `FieldDistanceCache` the evaluator owns. The nodes only build fields; Sample Field and Cull by Field are the two that turn them into numbers and culls, over a tree of placements, keeping paths.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6.4 (strict concurrency), Swift Testing, SwiftLint, the C++ OCCT shim (`COCCT`), `SeededRandom` (SplitMix64). MetalUI is not touched.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-fields-design.md` (binding; this plan is its §9 row "7b-1 core": §2.1, §2.2, §3.1 to §3.3, §4, §7 rows 1 to 3 and §8 for these parts), under `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-patterns-trees-design.md` (7a: trees, placements, culling, `SeededRandom`, `InstanceGrid`) with all its Errata, under `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-metalcreator-vertical-slice-design.md` with all its Errata. Baseline: master `8cf30f2`, 3,182 tests. File format stays **6**: a field is never saved.

**Not in this plan:** Image Field, images and format 7 (7b-2); Field Scatter, the previews, the theme roles and the acceptance test (7b-3). `Field`'s enum is shaped so they add to it without reshaping it: see "What 7b-2 and 7b-3 will extend".

## Global Constraints

- Swift 6 strict concurrency; Swift Testing (`@Test`, `#expect`), never XCTest; `@MainActor @Observable` models, behaviour in models and thin views.
- One type per file; no force unwraps and no force `try`; no `DispatchQueue` or other GCD; `Task.sleep(for:)` only; numbers in text through `FormatStyle` (`formatted(.number…)`), never `NumberFormatter`/`String(format:)`.
- Numbers in user-facing messages are written in `Locale.messages` (`en_US`): "1,000", "2.5".
- Kernel work stays behind the `Kernel` protocol; only `COCCT` and `CreatorOCCT` touch OCCT. Every OCCT call runs under `OCCTKernel.serialized`; every C allocation has a `*_free` (the distance shim allocates none: the output buffer is the caller's); no C++ exception crosses into Swift.
- Every `Kernel` operation checks `Task.checkCancellation()` on entry; the field code checks every 1,024 points.
- All randomness is `SeededRandom` under a pinned `Purpose` constant; never the system generator. A value never depends on the other points of a batch.
- Fields are computed, never saved; `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion` stays 6.
- Do not edit `Package.swift`'s MetalUI path; do not modify `../MetalUI`; never take screenshots. A MetalUI gap goes in `docs/metalui-gaps.md` (its entry and its summary-table row), is sent to the MetalUI session and is never worked around.
- `swiftlint lint --strict` reports zero violations. A task is not done with a new compiler warning.
- **A full `swift test` run passes only if** the exit code is 0, no line says "recorded an issue" or "failed after" (Swift Testing prints glyphs, not ✘), and 11 "Test run with" lines appear. Test counts below are "master + N" (master has 3,182) and are the sum of the "Test run with … tests" lines. **While `../MetalUI` is at `ff8a1ea4` or later (it is: see the next bullet) "passes" means the Gate 0 baseline and nothing more:** exit code 1, exactly two "recorded an issue" lines, both naming `ViewportCancelWindowTests.aPrimaryPressOnTheSameElementLeavesTheLostDragToTheNextDragValues`, exactly three "failed after" lines (that test, its suite and the one "Test run with" line that contains it, each "with 2 issues"), and 11 "Test run with" lines. A third issue, a second failing test or a different test is a failure.
- **MetalUI: do not touch it, and do not pretend it is `930db4a`.** `Package.swift` points at `../MetalUI`, which is at `ff8a1ea4` (MetalUI C26, PR #72), not the `930db4a` that `master` was built with. You may not edit or move `../MetalUI` or change `Package.swift`'s path, so the plan is built and judged against `ff8a1ea4`. Against it exactly one test is red on `master` itself, `ViewportCancelWindowTests.aPrimaryPressOnTheSameElementLeavesTheLostDragToTheNextDragValues` (2 issues: it pins the behaviour C26 fixed; it belongs to the track that adopts C26 and is not edited here). **Gate 0, before Task 1:** run `git -C ../MetalUI rev-parse --short HEAD` and the full suite once in this worktree, with no change made. Expected: `ff8a1ea4`, the baseline above, 3,182 tests (the same run with this plan applied, checked in a scratch copy, gave that baseline exactly, 3,401 tests). If it is anything else (`../MetalUI` was moved back to `930db4a` and the run is green, or another test is red), stop and report it before Task 1. Decision 12 offers the user two other ways out. The one compiler warning master already has (`ContextMenuTests.swift:153`, "'underPointer' mutated after capture") is not new.
- Messages follow the house style: plain sentences naming what to do ("Distance Field needs a target: wire a point, edges, a profile, faces or a solid.").

## Review Focus

The spec says what fields must do; it is silent on inputs like these, which a person using the software will meet. Each line names the input, what a reasonable person expects, and the test that pins it.

1. **A list or tree of fields wired into a node that works on a whole tree.** Sample Field and Cull by Field refuse in the layouts' words ("Sample Field takes single values, not lists: wire one value into each input, or pick one out of the list first with List Item.", `LayoutChecks.singleValues`), rather than the generic "works on a whole tree at once" message. The refusal runs before the node and cannot say which socket held the list, so it names none: a list on `level`, `seed`, `keep` or `mode` gets the same sentence, which is true of each. The spec (§2.1) allows a field per branch; the plan refuses it, as decision 11 says. Task 9: `aListOfFieldsIsRefusedInWords`, `aListInAnotherInputIsRefusedInTheSameWords` (both nodes).
2. **Points and numbers that are not finite.** Noise at 1e300 or ±∞ never traps and stays in 0…1 or NaN; the OCCT kernel refuses a NaN point as an error; Remap and Clamp refuse infinite ends in words. Tasks 5, 2, 8: `hugeAndOddCoordinatesNeverTrap`, `refusalsAreKernelErrors`, `remapRefusesRangeEndsThatAreNotFiniteNumbers`.
3. **Nothing to work on.** An empty point list gives no numbers and no kernel call; a tree whose branches are all culled gives an empty tree, not an error; an empty edge or face selection is the node's error, not a silent zero. Tasks 6, 9, 7: `noPointsGiveNoNumbersAndNoKernelCall`, `aTreeOfNothingGivesNothing`, `emptySelectionsBadPointsAndBlankProfilesAreRefusedInWords`.
4. **A superseded or failing evaluation.** A cancelled sampling stops with `CancellationError` and caches nothing, at the entry and again between chunks of 1,024 points; a kernel error is the sampling node's error with the kernel's own words, and is not cached. Tasks 6, 2, 4: `aCancelledSamplingStopsWithCancellationError` (the entry), `everyLoopStopsBetweenChunksWhenCancelled` (each of the three loops, counting the points they reached), `aSamplingCancelledInsideTheKernelStopsAtTheNextLoop` (a sampler running a batch), `aBatchCancelledBetweenChunksStopsThere` (the OCCT chunk loop, cancelled from a hook after the first chunk), `aCancelledMeasurementCachesNothing`, `aKernelErrorPassesThroughAndCachesNothing`.
5. **A solid that is gone, and a solid that is kept.** The distance cache keys on the solid object; an entry keeps its target alive, so a solid freed and a new one allocated at the same address is never mistaken for it. That also pins the solid (its OCCT shape and topology tables), so an entry's size counts `Solid.estimatedBytes` as well as its points and distances: a slider drag that makes a new solid per step cannot leave more than the cache's 64 MB behind, however few points each entry holds (the evaluator's own 512 MB budget does not see these solids; an entry that counts them twice over, when several entries share one solid, is a safe over-count). A hash collision costs a kernel call, never a wrong number (hits compare the points exactly). Task 4: `aDroppedSolidsIdentityIsNeverReusedByTheNextOne`, `anEntryCountsTheSolidItKeepsAlive`, `solidsKeptAliveByEntriesStayWithinTheBudget`.
6. **A field in a group's interface.** CLAUDE.md says any change older readers can't decode bumps `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion`, and a group interface socket of type `"field"` in a format-6 file is exactly that (an older build fails to open it with a generic read error, not the "saved by a newer version" message). 7b-2 owns format 7, so until then a field wire across a group's edge is refused in words and the word `"field"` never reaches a file (decision 8). A field chain wholly inside a group works. Task 3: `aFieldWireCrossingTheEdgeIsRefusedEitherWay`, `anInterfaceSocketOfTypeFieldIsRefused`, `nodesThatKeepTheFieldWireInsideGroupAndSaveAsFormatSix`.
7. **The graded-sizes chain has an order.** Remap takes and returns a field and there is no numeric Remap, so the chain that wires is Radial Gradient → **Remap → Sample Field** → a shortcut's size socket; the spec's "Sample Field → Remap → size" (§3.3, §8) cannot be wired (a Remap output into a number socket is `typeMismatch`, field → number). The plan says so in its code comments and prose, tests the chain on OCCT through a Hole Pattern, and Task 12 records an erratum in the spec so 7b-3's acceptance test is planned in the right order. Task 9: `gradedHolesFollowTheFieldAcrossThePlate`.
8. **OCCT distances on a solid that is not planes and cylinders.** The 100 ms target was set before any measurement on a non-analytic solid; on a smooth loft (B-spline faces) a point costs far more than on the plate. Task 2 pins batch = point by point on a loft too; Task 11 makes row 2 a gate that blocks the merge and adds a loft row (`field-2b`) that is recorded and not gated; `performance.md` says 100 ms is for analytic solids (decision 13).

## What 7b-2 and 7b-3 will extend

- **7b-2 (images)** adds one case to `Field` (`image`: a decoded `GreyImage`, a plane, a size, an edge mode). The compiler then lists exactly three places, because none of them has a `default`: `Field.children` (`Field+Structure.swift`), `Field.recipe(inside:)` (`Field+Description.swift`) and `Sampling.values(of:at:)` (`FieldSampler.swift`). `Field.estimatedBytes` gains the image's bytes. Its node (`ImageFieldNode`) goes in `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/Sources/` and in `BuiltInNodes`; `Scalar` and `SocketType` need nothing, because the image lives inside the `Field`. `GreyImageDecoder.swift` goes in `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/`. Format 7 touches `GraphFile`, `NodeSetting`, `ConstantValue`, `NodeClipboard` and the inspector, none of which this plan changes; it
also removes `GroupRefusal.fieldSocket` and its check in `GraphContent.checkInterface` (this plan's refusal of a field in a group's
interface, decision 8) and turns `GroupFieldTests` into round-trip tests, in the same commit that bumps the format.
- **7b-3 (scatter and previews)** needs no new `Field` case: previews call `FieldSampler.sample(_:at:kernel:cache:)` on a mesh's vertices with the evaluator's `fieldDistances` cache, which is why the cache is an actor on the evaluator and the sampler takes it as a parameter. Field Scatter (`Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/Using/FieldScatterNode.swift`) reads a field with the same call, draws with a new `SeededRandom.Purpose.scatter`, and is refused over 2,000 points by `PatternLimit`. The preview ramp's three theme roles go in `CreatorStyle` (`ThemeRole`, `ThemeColors`, `ColorTheme+*`), the tint in `CreatorViewport`'s `ViewportItem`, and `Palette.header(for: .fields)` may then use a role of its own.

## Decisions the plan makes for you (confirm or change before building)

The spec leaves these open. Each task is written for the recommended default; the alternative says what would change.

| # | Decision | Built (recommended) | Alternative and what it would change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The field socket's colour | Borrows the Feature header colour (orange in Dracula): `Palette.header(for: .fields)` | A `fieldHeader` theme role now: touches `ThemeColors`, `ThemeRole`, the three built-in themes, `.mctheme` and the theme editor, and collides with 7b-3's three ramp roles |
| 2 | A noise recipe | In full: `noise(seed 4, scale 5, octaves 3)` | The spec's example `noise(seed 4)`: shorter, hides what shapes the field |
| 3 | Remap's empty-range message | "Remap's from range is empty (both ends are 3)." | The spec's "… both ends are 3 mm": wrong for a gradient or noise, which have no unit |
| 4 | `FieldBench` row 3 | Sample Field + Cull by Field over 2,000 placements, same 50 ms; Field Scatter's row comes with 7b-3 | Drop row 3 here and leave it all to 7b-3 |
| 5 | Unsigned distance to a solid | The distance to its surface (a point inside is as far as its nearest wall) | 0 inside: needs a classifier call for every unsigned point too |
| 6 | Sample Field on one plane | A list of one number | A single number: needs `BroadcastPlan` to tell a single item from a list of one on a `tree` socket (a change to core evaluation) |
| 7 | Threshold on the level | Kept by Above and by Below | Strict on one side |
| 8 | A field wire across a group's edge | **Refused** until 7b-2's format 7: Group, "+" sockets and Edit Interface say "A field can't cross a group's edge yet: group the nodes on both sides of the wire together, or sample the field into numbers first." (CLAUDE.md: any change older readers can't decode bumps `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion`; a `"type":"field"` socket in a format-6 file would make an older build fail to open it with a generic read error). A field chain wholly inside a group, and numbers leaving it, work | Allow it under format 6: nothing is refused, but the file says `"field"` and an older build cannot open it (no "saved by a newer version" message); the plan would then test a field through a group instead (Distance Field inside, Remap outside, Group, Ungroup, Make Unique, save and open). Or bump to format 7 here, which collides with 7b-2 |
| 9 | Add and Multiply names | The spec's, so adding one reads "Undo Add Add" and "Undo Add Multiply" (both pinned in `FieldDisplayTests`, so the choice is visible) | "Add Fields" and "Multiply Fields" |
| 10 | Threads in the distance shim | `OSD_Parallel` blocks of 64 points under the process-wide lock | One thread: about the core count slower, likely over the 100 ms target on a plate |
| 11 | A list of fields into Sample Field or Cull by Field | Refused in the layouts' words (a `tree`-access socket cannot also broadcast, and a list on `level`, `seed`, `keep` or `mode` is refused the same way). The spec (§2.1) says a field per branch is allowed; only the maths nodes broadcast it | A field per branch: `field` becomes a `tree`-access socket and branch *k* of fields is sampled over branch *k* of the placements (a change to both nodes' evaluation, their tests and what an unequal count means) |
| 12 | `../MetalUI` is at `ff8a1ea4`, one test red on master | Gate 0 baseline: that one test's two issues are the only issues allowed, and the pass rule says so (Global Constraints) | (b) Fold C26's adoption into this plan: update `aPrimaryPressOnTheSameElementLeavesTheLostDragToTheNextDragValues` to the C26 behaviour (it belongs to the track that adopts C26; a clean `swift test` then returns). (c) You move `../MetalUI` back to `930db4a` yourself; the original rule (exit 0) holds |
| 13 | Row 2 (100 ms for 10,000 signed distances) is a merge gate | It blocks the merge on an idle Mac: pull the levers in order (planar fast path, coarser classifier tolerance), then come back to you; 100 ms is documented as the target for analytic solids (planes, cylinders), and a loft row (`field-2b`) is recorded, not gated | Record a miss and merge (parent spec §7.3: "recorded, never hidden"), or change the budget in the spec |

### Task 1: `Kernel.distances` and `DistanceTarget`, on `FakeKernel`

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorKernel/DistanceTarget.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorKernel/EdgeCurve+Distance.swift` (the line and arc maths, moved out of `RegionDistance`)
- Create: `Sources/CreatorKernel/FakeKernel+Distances.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorKernel/Kernel.swift`, `Sources/CreatorKernel/FakeKernel.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorOCCT/OCCTKernel.swift` (a placeholder that throws `unsupported`, so the package builds; Task 2 replaces it)
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Variation/RegionDistance.swift` (forward to the moved maths)
- Modify: `Tests/CreatorViewportTests/Support/Fixtures.swift` (`StubMeshKernel` gains the new requirement)
- Test: `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/FakeKernelDistanceTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/DistanceTargetTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Solid`, `FaceSet`, `EdgeSet`, `EdgeCurve`, `KernelError`, `BoundingBox` (all exist).
- Produces (later tasks rely on these exact names):
  - `public enum DistanceTarget: Sendable { case solid(Solid), faces(FaceSet), edges(EdgeSet) }` with
    `var solid: Solid`, `func validate() throws`, `var identity: DistanceTarget.Identity` (`Hashable, Sendable`).
  - `Kernel.distances(from points: [Vector3], to target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool) throws -> [Double]`.
  - `EdgeCurve.distance(from point: Vector3) -> Double`.
  - `FakeKernel` appends `"distances"` to `operationLog` on every call.

Semantics (fields spec §3.1, decisions D5): the distance is to the target's *surface* in millimetres. `signed` applies to
a `.solid` target only (negative inside, 0 on the surface); for `.faces` and `.edges` it is ignored. Unsigned distance
to a solid is the distance to its surface, so a point inside is as far from it as the nearest wall. `FakeKernel` is
exact for a solid's bounding box (the Fake's solids are boxes), for planar faces measured to their infinite plane
(`FaceInfo.normal` and `centroid`), and for straight or circular edges (`EdgeInfo.curve`); anything else throws
`KernelError.unsupported`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/FakeKernelDistanceTests.swift`:

```swift
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGeometry
@testable import CreatorKernel

/// Fields spec §3.1 and §8: `Kernel.distances` on the Fake. A 10 mm cube stands on z = 0, centred on x and y.
struct FakeKernelDistanceTests {
    let tag = NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0)

    func cube(_ kernel: FakeKernel) async throws -> Solid {
        try await kernel.extrude(.rectangle(width: 10, height: 10, plane: .xy), distance: 10, mode: .oneSided, tag: tag)
    }

    /// A solid whose faces are written by hand: a Fake prism's faces carry no positions.
    func solid(faces: [FaceInfo]) -> Solid {
        Solid(topology: Topology(faces: faces, edges: []), bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(1, 1, 1)), storage: FakeStorage())
    }

    func face(_ id: Int, kind: SurfaceKind = .plane, normal: Vector3?, at centroid: Vector3) -> FaceInfo {
        FaceInfo(id: FaceID(id), kind: kind, normal: normal, area: 1, centroid: centroid, tags: [])
    }

    // MARK: solids

    @Test func aCubesSignedDistanceIsMinusFiveAtItsCentreAndZeroOnAFace() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 5), Vector3(5, 0, 5), Vector3(0, 0, 10), Vector3(0, -5, 2)],
                                               to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(found == [-5, 0, 0, 0])
    }

    @Test func outsideTheCubeTheDistanceIsPositiveAndExact() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(8, 0, 5), Vector3(8, 8, 5), Vector3(8, 8, 13), Vector3(0, 0, -2)],
                                               to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(found[0] == 3)
        #expect(abs(found[1] - 18.0.squareRoot()) < 1e-12, "past an edge it is the distance to the edge")
        #expect(abs(found[2] - 27.0.squareRoot()) < 1e-12, "past a corner it is the distance to the corner")
        #expect(found[3] == 2)
    }

    @Test func unsignedIsTheDistanceToTheSurfaceWhicheverSideThePointIsOn() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 5), Vector3(1, 0, 5), Vector3(8, 0, 5)],
                                               to: .solid(solid), signed: false)
        #expect(found == [5, 4, 3])
    }

    @Test func aBatchGivesExactlyWhatEachPointGivesOnItsOwn() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let points = (0..<50).map { Vector3(Double($0) * 0.7 - 12, Double($0 % 7) - 3, Double($0 % 11) * 1.3 - 1) }
        let batch = try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        for (index, point) in points.enumerated() {
            let single = try await kernel.distances(from: [point], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
            #expect(single == [batch[index]])
        }
    }

    // MARK: edges

    @Test func anEdgesDistanceAtAKnownPointIsExact() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        // Edge 0 is the first segment's edge on the start cap: (-5,-5,0) to (5,-5,0).
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, -8, 4), Vector3(9, -5, 0), Vector3(0, -5, 0)],
                                               to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [EdgeID(0)])), signed: true)
        #expect(found == [5, 4, 0], "perpendicular to the edge, past its end, and on it; signed is ignored for edges")
    }

    @Test func severalEdgesGiveTheNearest() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let both = EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [EdgeID(0), EdgeID(1)])  // the start cap's and the end cap's edge of segment 0
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, -5, 8), Vector3(0, -5, 3)], to: .edges(both), signed: false)
        #expect(found == [2, 3])
    }

    @Test func anEdgeThatIsNotStraightOrCircularIsNotSupported() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        var topology = try await cube(kernel).topology
        topology.edges[0].curve = nil
        let bare = Solid(topology: topology, bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(1, 1, 1)), storage: FakeStorage())
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.unsupported("distance to an edge that isn't straight or circular")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: bare, edges: [EdgeID(0)])), signed: false)
        }
    }

    // MARK: faces

    @Test func aPlanarFaceIsMeasuredToItsPlane() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let plate = solid(faces: [face(0, normal: -.unitZ, at: .zero), face(1, normal: .unitZ, at: Vector3(0, 0, 6))])
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(1, 2, 3), Vector3(1, 2, -4)],
                                               to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: plate, faces: [FaceID(1)])), signed: true)
        #expect(found == [3, 10])
        let nearest = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(1, 2, 2)], to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: plate, faces: [FaceID(0), FaceID(1)])),
                                                 signed: false)
        #expect(nearest == [2])
    }

    @Test func aCurvedFaceIsNotSupported() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let round = solid(faces: [face(0, kind: .cylinder, normal: .unitZ, at: .zero)])
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.unsupported("distance to a curved face")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: round, faces: [FaceID(0)])), signed: false)
        }
    }

    // MARK: refusals and the contract

    @Test func anEmptySelectionAndAMissingIDAreRefused() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("No edges are selected to measure to.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [])), signed: false)
        }
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("No faces are selected to measure to.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: solid, faces: [])), signed: false)
        }
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("A selected edge doesn't exist on the solid.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [EdgeID(999)])), signed: false)
        }
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("A selected face doesn't exist on the solid.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: solid, faces: [FaceID(-1)])), signed: false)
        }
    }

    @Test func noPointsGiveNoDistances() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        #expect(try await kernel.distances(from: [], to: .solid(solid), signed: true).isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func eachCallIsLogged() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        await kernel.clearLog()
        _ = try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        _ = try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(await kernel.operationLog == ["distances", "distances"])
    }

    @Test func aCancelledCallIsSkipped() async throws {
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let task = Task {
            withUnsafeCurrentTask { $0?.cancel() }
            return try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        }
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorKernel/DistanceTarget.swift`:

```swift
/// What `Kernel.distances(from:to:signed:)` measures to (fields spec §3.1): a whole solid (its surface), some of a solid's
/// faces, or some of its edges.
public enum DistanceTarget: Sendable {
    case solid(Solid)
    case faces(FaceSet)
    case edges(EdgeSet)

    /// The solid the target belongs to.
    public var solid: Solid {
        switch self {
        case .solid(let solid): solid
        case .faces(let set): set.solid
        case .edges(let set): set.solid
        }
    }

    /// Refuses a target with nothing to measure to: an empty selection, or an ID the solid doesn't have. Every kernel
    /// calls it first, so the messages are the same everywhere.
    public func validate() throws {
        switch self {
        case .solid:
            return
        case .faces(let set):
            guard !set.faces.isEmpty else { throw KernelError.invalidInput("No faces are selected to measure to.") }
            let count = set.solid.topology.faces.count
            guard set.faces.allSatisfy({ (0..<count).contains($0.rawValue) }) else {
                throw KernelError.invalidInput("A selected face doesn't exist on the solid.")
            }
        case .edges(let set):
            guard !set.edges.isEmpty else { throw KernelError.invalidInput("No edges are selected to measure to.") }
            let count = set.solid.topology.edges.count
            guard set.edges.allSatisfy({ (0..<count).contains($0.rawValue) }) else {
                throw KernelError.invalidInput("A selected edge doesn't exist on the solid.")
            }
        }
    }

    /// Which target this is, for caches: the solid object (by identity), the kind, and the selected IDs as a sorted set. A
    /// cache that keys on it must keep the target itself alive, so the solid's identity can't be reused by another object.
    public struct Identity: Hashable, Sendable {
        let solid: ObjectIdentifier
        let kind: Int
        let ids: [Int]
    }

    public var identity: Identity {
        switch self {
        case .solid(let solid): Identity(solid: ObjectIdentifier(solid), kind: 0, ids: [])
        case .faces(let set): Identity(solid: ObjectIdentifier(set.solid), kind: 1, ids: Set(set.faces.map(\.rawValue)).sorted())
        case .edges(let set): Identity(solid: ObjectIdentifier(set.solid), kind: 2, ids: Set(set.edges.map(\.rawValue)).sorted())
        }
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorKernel/EdgeCurve+Distance.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

extension EdgeCurve {
    /// The distance from `point` to this edge: to the nearest point of a straight edge (an end, past it), or of an arc
    /// (straight to the circle where the point's angle about the axis is within the sweep, else to the nearer end). Moved
    /// here from Cull by Region so the Fake kernel and the nodes measure alike.
    public func distance(from point: Vector3) -> Double {
        switch self {
        case .line(let start, let end):
            let direction = end - start
            let lengthSquared = direction.dot(direction)
            guard lengthSquared > 0 else { return (point - start).length }
            let along = min(1, max(0, (point - start).dot(direction) / lengthSquared))
            return (point - (start + direction * along)).length
        case .circle(let center, let axis, let radius, let start, let sweep):
            return Self.distance(from: point, toCircle: center, axis: axis, radius: radius, start: start, sweep: sweep)
        }
    }

    private static func distance(from point: Vector3, toCircle center: Vector3, axis: Vector3, radius: Double, start: Vector3,
                                 sweep: Double) -> Double {
        guard let normal = axis.normalized, let first = (start - center).normalized else { return (point - center).length }
        let second = normal.cross(first)
        // `angle <= sweep` below assumes a positive (counter-clockwise) sweep, as OCCT reports it (cocct.h).
        assert(sweep >= 0, "arc sweep must be counter-clockwise positive")
        let offset = point - center
        let height = offset.dot(normal)
        let (x, y) = (offset.dot(first), offset.dot(second))
        var angle = atan2(y, x)
        if angle < 0 { angle += 2 * .pi }
        if sweep >= 2 * .pi - 1e-9 || angle <= sweep {
            let across = (x * x + y * y).squareRoot() - radius
            return (height * height + across * across).squareRoot()
        }
        let end = center + first * (radius * cos(sweep)) + second * (radius * sin(sweep))
        return min((point - start).length, (point - end).length)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorKernel/FakeKernel+Distances.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

extension FakeKernel {
    /// The Fake's distances (fields spec §3.1): exact for a box (the Fake's solids are bounding boxes), for planar faces
    /// measured to their infinite plane, and for straight or circular edges. Everything else is unsupported.
    static func measure(_ points: [Vector3], to target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool) throws -> [Double] {
        try target.validate()
        switch target {
        case .solid(let solid):
            return points.map { boxDistance($0, solid.bounds, signed: signed) }
        case .faces(let set):
            let found = Set(set.faces.map(\.rawValue)).sorted().compactMap { set.solid.topology.face(FaceID($0)) }
            let planes = found.compactMap { face -> (origin: Vector3, normal: Vector3)? in
                guard face.kind == .plane, let normal = face.normal?.normalized else { return nil }
                return (face.centroid, normal)
            }
            guard planes.count == found.count else { throw KernelError.unsupported("distance to a curved face") }
            return points.map { point in planes.map { abs($0.normal.dot(point - $0.origin)) }.min() ?? .infinity }
        case .edges(let set):
            let found = Set(set.edges.map(\.rawValue)).sorted().compactMap { set.solid.topology.edge(EdgeID($0)) }
            let curves = found.compactMap(\.curve)
            guard curves.count == found.count else {
                throw KernelError.unsupported("distance to an edge that isn't straight or circular")
            }
            return points.map { point in curves.map { $0.distance(from: point) }.min() ?? .infinity }
        }
    }

    /// The distance to a box's surface: negative inside when `signed`, else its size.
    static func boxDistance(_ point: Vector3, _ box: BoundingBox, signed: Bool) -> Double {
        let center = box.center
        let half = box.size * 0.5
        let (qx, qy, qz) = (abs(point.x - center.x) - half.x, abs(point.y - center.y) - half.y, abs(point.z - center.z) - half.z)
        let outside = Vector3(max(qx, 0), max(qy, 0), max(qz, 0)).length
        let distance = outside + min(max(qx, max(qy, qz)), 0)
        return signed ? distance : abs(distance)
    }
}
```

```swift
    /// Volume, surface area and centroid. Used by inspectors and the conformance suite.
    func properties(of solid: Solid) throws -> SolidProperties
    /// The distance in millimetres from each of `points` to `target`, in the order of `points` (fields spec §3.1). To a
    /// `.solid` it is to the surface: with `signed`, negative inside and 0 on it; unsigned it is the size of that (so a
    /// point inside is as far from the solid as its nearest wall). `signed` is ignored for `.faces` and `.edges`. Empty
    /// `points` give no distances; an empty or dangling selection is `KernelError.invalidInput` (`DistanceTarget.validate`).
    /// Like every operation it checks for cancellation on entry, and a long batch again between chunks.
    func distances(from points: [Vector3], to target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool) throws -> [Double]
}
```

```swift
    static func distance(from point: Vector3, to curve: EdgeCurve) -> Double {
        switch curve {
        case .line(let start, let end):
            let direction = end - start
            let lengthSquared = direction.dot(direction)
            guard lengthSquared > 0 else { return (point - start).length }
            let along = min(1, max(0, (point - start).dot(direction) / lengthSquared))
            return (point - (start + direction * along)).length
        case .circle(let center, let axis, let radius, let start, let sweep):
            return distance(from: point, toCircle: center, axis: axis, radius: radius, start: start, sweep: sweep)
        }
    }

    /// To an arc: straight to the circle where the point's angle about the axis is within the sweep, else to the nearer end.
    private static func distance(from point: Vector3, toCircle center: Vector3, axis: Vector3, radius: Double, start: Vector3,
                                 sweep: Double) -> Double {
        guard let normal = axis.normalized, let first = (start - center).normalized else { return (point - center).length }
        let second = normal.cross(first)
        // `angle <= sweep` below assumes a positive (counter-clockwise) sweep, as OCCT reports it (cocct.h).
        assert(sweep >= 0, "arc sweep must be counter-clockwise positive")
        let offset = point - center
        let height = offset.dot(normal)
        let (x, y) = (offset.dot(first), offset.dot(second))
        var angle = atan2(y, x)
        if angle < 0 { angle += 2 * .pi }
        if sweep >= 2 * .pi - 1e-9 || angle <= sweep {
            let across = (x * x + y * y).squareRoot() - radius
            return (height * height + across * across).squareRoot()
        }
        let end = center + first * (radius * cos(sweep)) + second * (radius * sin(sweep))
        return min((point - start).length, (point - end).length)
    }
}
```

### Task 2: `Kernel.distances` on OCCT

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/COCCT/cocct_distance.cpp`
- Modify: `Sources/COCCT/include/cocct.h`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorOCCT/OCCTShape+Distance.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorOCCT/OCCTKernel.swift` (replaces Task 1's placeholder)
- Test: `Tests/CreatorOCCTTests/DistanceConformanceTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `DistanceTarget` and `Kernel.distances` (Task 1); `OCCTKernel.serialized`, `OCCTKernel.shape(of:)`, `OCCTShape.check`, `KernelError.occt(_:_:)`.
- Produces: `occt_distances` (C), `OCCTShape.distances(from:kind:indices:signed:)`, and the working `OCCTKernel.distances(from:to:signed:)`
  (chunks of 1,024 points with `Task.checkCancellation()` between them) over an internal
  `distances(from:to:signed:afterChunk:)` whose hook runs after each chunk, so a test can cancel between two chunks
  deterministically.

How it measures: a target made of faces (the whole solid's faces for `.solid`, the picked faces for `.faces`) or of edges
is collected into a compound. The points are cut into blocks of 64 that OCCT's thread pool (`OSD_Parallel`) shares out; each
block loads the compound once as the second shape of its own `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` and loads each point as the first
shape (a vertex) in turn. The threads share only the shapes, which they read and never change, and the process-wide lock
the caller holds (`OCCTKernel.serialized`) keeps every other OCCT call out meanwhile; no exception leaves a worker (a block
that throws records its message and the others stop), and a block's objects are stack-owned, so nothing is leaked. Measuring to the faces, not to the solid, matters: OCCT answers 0 for a point
inside a solid it is given, whereas Distance Field wants the distance to the surface. The sign of a solid distance comes
from one `BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier` per block (`TopAbs_IN` gives a negative distance), asked only for a point inside the
solid's bounding box: a point outside the box cannot be inside the solid. Every C++ object is stack or RAII; the
output buffer is the caller's, so the shim allocates nothing to free; every exception becomes the status, as in every
other shim call (`cocct::guarded`). Faces and edges are the *bounded* sub-shapes, so a point beside a small face is
measured to the face's nearest edge, which the Fake (infinite planes) does not do; the tests say so. What a point costs depends on
the faces: on planes and cylinders it is tens of microseconds, on a smooth loft's B-spline faces it was measured at about 0.9 ms
a point on a saturated machine, so the spec's 100 ms for 10,000 points is a target for analytic solids only (decision 13, Task 11).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `Tests/CreatorOCCTTests/DistanceConformanceTests.swift`:

```swift
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGeometry
@testable import CreatorKernel
@testable import CreatorOCCT

/// Fields spec §3.1 and §8 on OCCT: a 10 mm cube standing on z = 0, centred on x and y.
struct DistanceConformanceTests {
    func near(_ a: Double, _ b: Double, tolerance: Double = 1e-7) -> Bool { abs(a - b) <= tolerance }

    func cube(_ kernel: any Kernel, tag: NodeTag = newTag()) async throws -> Solid {
        try await box(kernel, 10, 10, 10, tag: tag)
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aCubesSignedDistanceIsMinusFiveAtItsCentreAndZeroOnAFace(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 5), Vector3(5, 0, 5), Vector3(0, 0, 10), Vector3(8, 0, 5)],
                                               to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(near(found[0], -5) && near(found[1], 0) && near(found[2], 0) && near(found[3], 3), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func unsignedIsTheDistanceToTheSurfaceFromEitherSide(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 5), Vector3(1, 0, 5), Vector3(8, 0, 5)], to: .solid(solid),
                                               signed: false)
        #expect(near(found[0], 5) && near(found[1], 4) && near(found[2], 3), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aCylindersDistanceIsExactOnACurvedSurface(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await kernel.extrude(.circle(radius: 3, center: .zero, plane: .xy), distance: 10, mode: .oneSided, tag: newTag())
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 5), Vector3(5, 0, 5), Vector3(0, 4, 5)], to: .solid(solid),
                                               signed: true)
        #expect(near(found[0], -3) && near(found[1], 2) && near(found[2], 1), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aBatchAcrossSeveralChunksAgreesWithTheFakesBoxMaths(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        var points: [Vector3] = []
        for x in -6...6 {
            for y in -6...6 {
                for z in -3...9 { points.append(Vector3(Double(x) * 1.3, Double(y) * 1.1, Double(z) * 1.7)) }
            }
        }
        #expect(points.count > 2 * 1024, "the batch spans three chunks")
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(found.count == points.count)
        var worst = 0.0
        for (point, distance) in zip(points, found) {
            worst = max(worst, abs(distance - FakeKernel.boxDistance(point, solid.bounds, signed: true)))
        }
        #expect(worst < 1e-7, "OCCT and the box formula differ by \(worst)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aBatchGivesWhatEachPointGivesOnItsOwn(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        // 200 points: three blocks of 64 and a part, which the shim's threads share out.
        let points = (0..<200).map { Vector3(Double($0) * 0.13 - 14, Double($0 % 7) - 3, Double($0 % 11) * 1.3 - 1) }
        let batch = try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        for (index, point) in points.enumerated() {
            let single = try await kernel.distances(from: [point], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
            #expect(near(single[0], batch[index], tolerance: 1e-12))
        }
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aPointInTheBoundsButOutsideTheShapeIsStillOutside(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        // R4 corners: the corner arc is centred on (26, 16), so (29, 19) is inside the bounding box but 0.243 outside the plate,
        // and (28, 18) is 1.172 inside it.
        let plate = try await kernel.extrude(.roundedRectangle(width: 60, height: 40, radius: 4, plane: .xy), distance: 6,
                                             mode: .oneSided, tag: newTag())
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(29, 19, 3), Vector3(28, 18, 3)], to: .solid(plate), signed: true)
        #expect(near(found[0], 4.242640687119285 - 4) && near(found[1], 2.8284271247461903 - 4), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func anEdgesDistanceAtAKnownPoint(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        // The cube's bottom edge along x at y = -5, z = 0.
        let edge = try #require(solid.topology.edges.first { edge in
            guard case .line(let start, let end)? = edge.curve else { return false }
            return abs(start.y + 5) < 1e-9 && abs(start.z) < 1e-9 && abs(end.y + 5) < 1e-9 && abs(end.z) < 1e-9
        })
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, -8, 4), Vector3(9, -5, 0), Vector3(0, -5, 0)],
                                               to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [edge.id])), signed: true)
        #expect(near(found[0], 5) && near(found[1], 4) && near(found[2], 0), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aFacesDistanceIsToTheBoundedFace(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let tag = newTag()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel, tag: tag)
        let top = try #require(faces(solid, role: .endCap, of: tag).first)
        let target = DistanceTarget.faces(FaceSet(solid: solid, faces: [top.id]))
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(1, 2, 3), Vector3(20, 0, 10)], to: target, signed: true)
        #expect(near(found[0], 7), "straight down onto the face")
        #expect(near(found[1], 15), "beside the face it is the distance to its nearest edge, 15 mm, not to its plane")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func severalFacesGiveTheNearest(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let tag = newTag()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel, tag: tag)
        let both = faces(solid, role: .startCap, of: tag) + faces(solid, role: .endCap, of: tag)
        let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, 3), Vector3(0, 0, 8)],
                                               to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: solid, faces: both.map(\.id))), signed: false)
        #expect(near(found[0], 3) && near(found[1], 2), "got \(found)")
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func refusalsAreKernelErrors(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("No faces are selected to measure to.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .faces(FaceSet(solid: solid, faces: [])), signed: false)
        }
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("A selected edge doesn't exist on the solid.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [EdgeID(999)])), signed: false)
        }
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("A point to measure from is not a finite position.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(.nan, 0, 0)], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        }
        let foreign = try await FakeKernel().extrude(.rectangle(width: 1, height: 1, plane: .xy), distance: 1, mode: .oneSided,
                                                     tag: newTag())
        await #expect(throws: KernelError.invalidInput("This solid was made by a different kernel.")) {
            try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .solid(foreign), signed: true)
        }
        #expect(try await kernel.distances(from: [], to: .solid(solid), signed: true).isEmpty)
    }

    @Test(arguments: KernelUnderTest.allCases)
    func aCancelledCallIsSkipped(_ under: KernelUnderTest) async throws {
        let kernel = under.make()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let task = Task {
            withUnsafeCurrentTask { $0?.cancel() }
            return try await kernel.distances(from: [.zero], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        }
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }

    /// Cancelled from a hook after the first of three chunks: the loop's own check, not the one on entry, stops it.
    @Test func aBatchCancelledBetweenChunksStopsThere() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        let points = (0..<(3 * OCCTKernel.distanceChunk)).map { Vector3(Double($0 % 17) - 8, 0, 5) }
        let task = Task {
            try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true, afterChunk: {
                withUnsafeCurrentTask { $0?.cancel() }
            })
        }
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
        let whole = try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(whole.count == points.count, "the same batch without the cancel runs to the end")
    }

    /// A smooth loft through three circles has B-spline faces, not the planes and cylinders of the other fixtures: the shim's
    /// threads read a different kind of geometry, and a batch must still equal each point on its own, exactly.
    @Test func aBatchOnALoftGivesWhatEachPointGivesOnItsOwn() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let sections = [(0.0, 5.0), (10, 3), (20, 6)].map { height, radius in
            Profile2D.circle(radius: radius, center: .zero, plane: Plane.xy.offset(by: height))
        }
        let solid = try await kernel.loft(sections, ruled: false, tag: newTag())
        // 300 points in and around the loft: four blocks of 64 and a part for the threads to share out.
        let points = (0..<300).map { Vector3(Double($0 % 13) - 6, Double($0 % 7) * 1.5 - 4.5, Double($0) * 0.08 - 1) }
        let batch = try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
        #expect(batch.contains { $0 < 0 } && batch.contains { $0 > 0 }, "points on both sides of the surface")
        for (index, point) in points.enumerated() {
            let single = try await kernel.distances(from: [point], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
            #expect(near(single[0], batch[index], tolerance: 1e-12), "point \(index)")
        }
        #expect(try await kernel.distances(from: points, to: .solid(solid), signed: true) == batch, "a second run gives the same numbers")
    }

    /// The shim itself, below the Swift checks: a face index it doesn't have is a status, never an exception out of C++.
    @Test func aBadIndexInTheShimIsAnErrorNotACrash() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let shape = try await kernel.shape(of: try await cube(kernel))
        #expect(throws: OCCTError(message: "face index out of range")) {
            _ = try shape.distances(from: [Vector3.zero][...], kind: .faces, indices: [99], signed: false)
        }
        #expect(throws: OCCTError(message: "edge index out of range")) {
            _ = try shape.distances(from: [Vector3.zero][...], kind: .edges, indices: [0], signed: false)
        }
        #expect(throws: OCCTError(message: "nothing to measure to")) {
            _ = try shape.distances(from: [Vector3.zero][...], kind: .edges, indices: [], signed: false)
        }
    }

    @Test func manyCallsLeakNothingVisibleAndKeepWorking() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let solid = try await cube(kernel)
        for round in 0..<200 {
            let found = try await kernel.distances(from: [Vector3(0, 0, Double(round % 10))], to: .solid(solid), signed: true)
            #expect(found.count == 1)
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `Sources/COCCT/cocct_distance.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "cocct_internal.hpp"

#include <BRepBndLib.hxx>
#include <BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex.hxx>
#include <BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier.hxx>
#include <BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape.hxx>
#include <BRep_Builder.hxx>
#include <Bnd_Box.hxx>
#include <OSD_Parallel.hxx>
#include <TopAbs_State.hxx>
#include <TopoDS_Compound.hxx>
#include <TopoDS_Vertex.hxx>
#include <gp_Pnt.hxx>

#include <algorithm>
#include <atomic>
#include <mutex>
#include <string>

using namespace cocct;

namespace {

/// The distances from a run of points to one target, in blocks that OCCT's own thread pool shares out (`OSD_Parallel`). Each
/// block builds its own extrema and classifier objects, so the threads share only the shapes, which they read and never
/// change; the process-wide lock the caller holds keeps every other OCCT call out meanwhile. No exception leaves a worker: a
/// block that throws records its message and the rest stop.
struct DistanceBlocks {
    static constexpr int block = 64;

    const TopoDS_Shape &target;
    const TopoDS_Shape &solid;
    const double *points;
    double *out;
    int count;
    bool signed_solid;
    Bnd_Box bounds;
    mutable std::atomic<bool> failed{false};
    mutable std::mutex lock;
    mutable std::string message;

    int blocks() const { return (count + block - 1) / block; }

    void fail(const std::string &text) const {
        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> guard(lock);
        if (!failed.exchange(true)) {
            message = text;
        }
    }

    void operator()(int index) const {
        if (failed.load()) {
            return;
        }
        try {
            BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape distance;
            distance.LoadS2(target);
            BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier classifier;
            bool loaded = false;
            const int end = std::min(count, (index + 1) * block);
            for (int i = index * block; i < end; ++i) {
                const gp_Pnt point(points[3 * i], points[3 * i + 1], points[3 * i + 2]);
                distance.LoadS1(BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex(point).Vertex());
                if (!distance.Perform() || !distance.IsDone()) {
                    throw user_error("OCCT could not measure the distance from a point");
                }
                double value = distance.Value();
                // Only a point inside the solid's bounds can be inside the solid; the others skip the classifier.
                if (signed_solid && !bounds.IsOut(point)) {
                    if (!loaded) {
                        classifier.Load(solid);
                        loaded = true;
                    }
                    classifier.Perform(point, 1e-7);
                    if (classifier.State() == TopAbs_IN) {
                        value = -value;
                    }
                }
                out[i] = value;
            }
        } catch (const user_error &error) {
            fail(error.what());
        } catch (const Standard_Failure &failure) {
            const char *text = failure.GetMessageString();
            fail(std::string("occt: ") + ((text && *text) ? text : "unknown exception"));
        } catch (const std::exception &error) {
            fail(std::string("occt: ") + error.what());
        } catch (...) {
            fail("occt: unknown exception");
        }
    }
};

} // namespace

extern "C" {

int occt_distances(const occt_shape *shape, int kind, const int *indices, int index_count, const double *points,
                   int point_count, int signed_distance, double *out, occt_status *status) {
    return guarded(status, [&]() -> int {
        if (!shape || !points || !out || point_count < 0) {
            set_error(status, "there are no points to measure from");
            return 0;
        }
        // The target: every face of the shape, or the picked faces or edges (1-based, in the indexed maps).
        const bool edges = kind == 2;
        const TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape all = map_of(shape->shape, edges ? TopAbs_EDGE : TopAbs_FACE);
        TopoDS_Compound target;
        BRep_Builder builder;
        builder.MakeCompound(target);
        if (kind == 0) {
            for (int i = 1; i <= all.Extent(); ++i) {
                builder.Add(target, all(i));
            }
        } else {
            if (!indices || index_count <= 0) {
                throw user_error("nothing to measure to");
            }
            for (int i = 0; i < index_count; ++i) {
                if (indices[i] < 1 || indices[i] > all.Extent()) {
                    throw user_error(edges ? "edge index out of range" : "face index out of range");
                }
                builder.Add(target, all(indices[i]));
            }
        }
        // The sign of a solid's distance: inside is negative. Faces and edges are never signed.
        const bool signed_solid = signed_distance != 0 && kind == 0;
        DistanceBlocks job{target, shape->shape, points, out, point_count, signed_solid, Bnd_Box()};
        if (signed_solid) {
            BRepBndLib::AddOptimal(shape->shape, job.bounds, Standard_False, Standard_False);
            job.bounds.Enlarge(1e-6);
        }
        OSD_Parallel::For(0, job.blocks(), job, job.blocks() < 2);
        if (job.failed.load()) {
            throw user_error(job.message.c_str());
        }
        return 1;
    });
}

} // extern "C"
```

Edit `Sources/COCCT/include/cocct.h`, replace:

```c
/// A compound of copies-by-reference of the given shapes.
occt_shape *occt_make_compound(const occt_shape *const *shapes, int count, occt_status *status);
```

with:

```c
/// Distances in millimetres from `point_count` points (x, y, z triples in `points`) to part of `shape`, written to
/// `out` (one per point; the caller owns it, the shim allocates nothing). `kind` 0 measures to the surface of the whole
/// shape (every face), 1 to the faces and 2 to the edges at the 1-based `indices` (OCCT's indexed face and edge maps,
/// as in occt_fillet). With `signed_distance` and kind 0, a point inside the shape gets a negative distance. Returns 1.
int occt_distances(const occt_shape *shape, int kind, const int *indices, int index_count, const double *points,
                   int point_count, int signed_distance, double *out, occt_status *status);
/// A compound of copies-by-reference of the given shapes.
occt_shape *occt_make_compound(const occt_shape *const *shapes, int count, occt_status *status);
```

Create `Sources/CreatorOCCT/OCCTShape+Distance.swift`:

```swift
import COCCT
import CreatorGeometry

extension OCCTShape {
    /// What `distances(from:kind:indices:signed:)` measures to (`occt_distances`' `kind`).
    enum DistanceKind: Int32 {
        /// The surface of the whole shape.
        case surface = 0
        /// The faces at the given 1-based indices.
        case faces = 1
        /// The edges at the given 1-based indices.
        case edges = 2
    }

    /// One distance per point, in order (see `occt_distances`). Call under `OCCTKernel.serialized`.
    func distances(from points: ArraySlice<Vector3>, kind: DistanceKind, indices: [Int32],
                   signed: Bool) throws(OCCTError) -> [Double] {
        guard !points.isEmpty else { return [] }
        var coordinates: [Double] = []
        coordinates.reserveCapacity(points.count * 3)
        for point in points {
            coordinates.append(point.x)
            coordinates.append(point.y)
            coordinates.append(point.z)
        }
        var result = [Double](repeating: 0, count: points.count)
        let count = Int32(points.count)
        try Self.check { status in
            indices.withUnsafeBufferPointer { ids in
                coordinates.withUnsafeBufferPointer { xyz in
                    result.withUnsafeMutableBufferPointer { out in
                        occt_distances(self.raw, kind.rawValue, ids.baseAddress, Int32(ids.count), xyz.baseAddress, count,
                                       signed ? 1 : 0, out.baseAddress, status)
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        return result
    }
}
```

```swift

```

with:

```swift
    /// How many points one call into the shim measures; the batch checks for cancellation between chunks.
    static let distanceChunk = 1024

    /// Distances to a solid's surface, faces or edges (fields spec §3.1): `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` from each point and,
    /// for a signed solid distance, a `BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier` for the sign, under `serialized`.
    public func distances(from points: [Vector3], to target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool) throws -> [Double] {
        try distances(from: points, to: target, signed: signed, afterChunk: {})
    }

    /// `distances(from:to:signed:)` with a hook that runs after each chunk is measured, before the loop's next check for
    /// cancellation. The conformance tests cancel from it to prove the loop stops between chunks.
    func distances(from points: [Vector3], to target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool,
                   afterChunk: @Sendable () -> Void) throws -> [Double] {
        try Task.checkCancellation()
        try target.validate()
        guard points.allSatisfy(\.isFinite) else {
            throw KernelError.invalidInput("A point to measure from is not a finite position.")
        }
        let source = try shape(of: target.solid)
        let kind: OCCTShape.DistanceKind
        let indices: [Int32]
        switch target {
        case .solid:
            (kind, indices) = (.surface, [])
        case .faces(let set):
            (kind, indices) = (.faces, set.faces.map { Int32($0.rawValue + 1) })
        case .edges(let set):
            (kind, indices) = (.edges, set.edges.map { Int32($0.rawValue + 1) })
        }
        var result: [Double] = []
        result.reserveCapacity(points.count)
        var start = 0
        while start < points.count {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let chunk = points[start..<min(start + Self.distanceChunk, points.count)]
            do {
                // Untyped throws on purpose: Swift 6.4 IRGen crashes with typed throws in this closure. Revert once fixed.
                result += try Self.serialized { () throws -> [Double] in
                    try source.distances(from: chunk, kind: kind, indices: indices, signed: signed)
                }
            } catch let error as OCCTError {
                throw KernelError.occt("distance", error)
            }
            start += chunk.count
            afterChunk()
        }
        return result
    }
```

### Task 3: The `Field` value, `.field` sockets, the conversion and the recipe text

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field.swift` (the enum and its distance sources)
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Structure.swift` (`children`, `nodeCount`, `estimatedBytes`)
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Description.swift` (the recipe text)
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Scalar.swift`, `SocketType.swift`, `SocketType+IndefiniteName.swift`, `NodeCategory.swift`, `NodeInputs.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorEditor/Palette.swift` (the socket colour role)
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Groups/GroupRefusal.swift`, `Sources/CreatorGraph/Groups/GraphContent+Apply.swift` (a group interface refuses a field socket; decision 8)
- Modify: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Trees/AnySocketTests.swift` (the category list)
- Test: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/FieldValueTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/FieldDescriptionTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/FieldPaletteTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Groups/GroupFieldTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `DistanceTarget` (Task 1), `Plane`, `Vector3`, `Profile2D`, `Locale.messages`, `Double.millimetreText(locale:)`.
- Produces (later tasks use these names exactly):
  - `public indirect enum Field: Sendable` with cases `constant(Double)`, `distance(DistanceSource, signed: Bool)`,
    `linearGradient(plane: Plane, length: Double)`, `radialGradient(center: Vector3, radius: Double)`,
    `noise(seed: Int, scale: Double, octaves: Int)`, `add(Field, Field)`, `multiply(Field, Field)`, `minimum(Field, Field)`,
    `maximum(Field, Field)`, `remap(Field, fromLow:, fromHigh:, toLow:, toHigh:, clamped:)`, `clamp(Field, low:, high:)`,
    `smoothMinimum(Field, Field, width:)`; `Field.DistanceSource` = `.point(Vector3)`, `.profile(Profile2D)`, `.kernel(DistanceTarget)`.
  - `Field.children: [Field]`, `Field.nodeCount: Int`, `Field.estimatedBytes: Int`, `Field.description: String`,
    `Field.description(limit: Int) -> String`.
  - `Scalar.field(Field)`, `SocketType.field`, `NodeCategory.fields`, `NodeInputs.field(_:) throws -> Field`.

Design for the tracks after this one: every `switch` over `Field` that must know each case is written without a `default`
(`children`, `description`, and the sampler in Task 6), so 7b-2 adding `case image(...)` is a compile error at exactly
those three places and nowhere else, and 7b-3's preview sampling needs no new case at all (it calls `FieldSampler`).
Fields are never saved: `Field` is not `Codable`, and under format 6 nothing may write the word `"field"` either. A group
interface socket of type `field` would store it (`SocketType` is a string enum), and a format-6 file holding it fails to decode in
an older build with a generic read error instead of the "saved by a newer version" message. CLAUDE.md says a change older
readers can't decode bumps `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion`, and that bump is 7b-2's, so until then `GraphContent.apply` refuses a
`.field` socket in any group interface (`GroupRefusal.fieldSocket`: Group, the "+" sockets and Edit Interface all pass through
`checkInterface`), and a field chain kept wholly inside a group is unaffected (decision 8). 7b-2 deletes the refusal when it
bumps the format.

Create `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/FieldValueTests.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// Fields spec §2.1: `Scalar.field`, `SocketType.field`, the one-way conversion from numbers, and what the evaluator does with it.
struct FieldValueTests {
    // MARK: the type and the conversion (D4)

    @Test func aFieldIsAFieldTypedValue() {
        #expect(Scalar.field(.constant(1)).type == .field)
        #expect(SocketType.field.indefiniteName == "a field")
        #expect(SocketType.field.rawValue == "field")
    }

    @Test func aNumberOrAnIntegerBecomesAConstantField() throws {
        let fromNumber = try #require(Scalar.number(2.5).converted(to: .field))
        let fromInteger = try #require(Scalar.integer(3).converted(to: .field))
        guard case .field(let a) = fromNumber, case .field(let b) = fromInteger else {
            Issue.record("expected fields")
            return
        }
        #expect(a.description == "2.5" && b.description == "3")
    }

    @Test func nothingConvertsBackToANumberOrAnythingElse() {
        let field = Scalar.field(.constant(1))
        for target in [SocketType.number, .integer, .bool, .vector, .plane, .profile, .solid, .edgeSet, .faceSet] {
            #expect(field.converted(to: target) == nil, "a field is not \(target)")
        }
        #expect(field.converted(to: .field) != nil)
        #expect(field.converted(to: .any) != nil)
    }

    @Test func otherTypesDoNotBecomeFields() {
        #expect(Scalar.bool(true).converted(to: .field) == nil)
        #expect(Scalar.vector(.zero).converted(to: .field) == nil)
        #expect(Scalar.plane(.xy).converted(to: .field) == nil)
    }

    @Test func aFieldSocketAcceptsNumbersAndIntegersButNothingAcceptsAField() {
        #expect(SocketType.field.accepts(.number) && SocketType.field.accepts(.integer) && SocketType.field.accepts(.field))
        #expect(!SocketType.field.accepts(.bool) && !SocketType.field.accepts(.vector) && !SocketType.field.accepts(.solid))
        for type in SocketType.allCases where type != .field && type != .any {
            #expect(!type.accepts(.field), "\(type) must not accept a field")
        }
        #expect(SocketType.any.accepts(.field) && SocketType.field.accepts(.any))
    }

    @Test func aListOfNumbersConvertsToAListOfFields() throws {
        let list = try #require(Value.list([.number(1), .integer(2), .number(0.5)]).converted(to: .field))
        #expect(list.items.map { if case .field(let f) = $0 { f.description } else { "?" } } == ["1", "2", "0.5"])
        #expect(Value.list([.number(1), .bool(true)]).converted(to: .field) == nil)
    }

    // MARK: size

    @Test func aFieldCostsSixtyFourPlusThirtyTwoPerNode() {
        #expect(Field.constant(1).nodeCount == 1 && Field.constant(1).estimatedBytes == 96)
        let tree = Field.add(.constant(1), .multiply(.constant(2), .constant(3)))
        #expect(tree.nodeCount == 5 && tree.estimatedBytes == 64 + 32 * 5)
        #expect(Scalar.field(tree).estimatedBytes == tree.estimatedBytes)
        #expect(Value.one(.field(tree)).estimatedBytes == 16 + tree.estimatedBytes)
    }

    @Test func childrenAreTheOperandsInOrder() {
        let a = Field.constant(1), b = Field.noise(seed: 1, scale: 1, octaves: 1)
        #expect(Field.add(a, b).children.count == 2)
        #expect(Field.remap(a, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 1, toLow: 0, toHigh: 1, clamped: true).children.count == 1)
        #expect(Field.radialGradient(center: .zero, radius: 1).children.isEmpty)
        #expect(Field.smoothMinimum(a, b, width: 1).children.count == 2)
    }

    // MARK: through the evaluator

    static let registry = NodeRegistry([
        FieldSourceNode.self, FieldSinkNode.self, NumberSourceNode.self, WholeSourceNode.self, NumberSinkNode.self, NumberListNode.self,
    ])

    func run(_ nodes: [Node], _ links: [Link], demand: Node) async throws -> NodeResult {
        let graph = Graph(nodes: Dictionary(uniqueKeysWithValues: nodes.map { ($0.id, $0) }), links: links)
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: Self.registry, kernel: FakeKernel()).evaluate(graph, demand: [demand.id])
        return try #require(report.results[demand.id])
    }

    /// A wire from `source`'s `socket` into the sink's "field".
    func wire(_ source: Node, _ socket: SocketName, to sink: Node) -> Link {
        Link(from: Endpoint(node: source.id, socket: socket), to: Endpoint(node: sink.id, socket: "field"))
    }

    func recipe(_ result: NodeResult) -> String? {
        if case .one(.field(let field))? = result.outputs?["field"] { field.description } else { nil }
    }

    @Test func anUnwiredFieldSocketTakesItsNumberAsAConstantField() async throws {
        let sink = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSinkNode.typeID)
        #expect(recipe(try await run([sink], [], demand: sink)) == "7")
        var typed = sink
        typed.inputValues["field"] = .number(0.5)
        #expect(recipe(try await run([typed], [], demand: typed)) == "0.5", "a number typed into a field socket (Multiply by 0.5)")
    }

    @Test func aNumberOrIntegerWireArrivesAsAConstantField() async throws {
        let number = Self.registry.makeNode(NumberSourceNode.typeID)
        let whole = Self.registry.makeNode(WholeSourceNode.typeID)
        let sink = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSinkNode.typeID)
        let fromNumber = try await run([number, sink], [wire(number, "value", to: sink)], demand: sink)
        let fromWhole = try await run([whole, sink], [wire(whole, "value", to: sink)], demand: sink)
        #expect(recipe(fromNumber) == "4.5" && recipe(fromWhole) == "6")
    }

    @Test func aFieldWireIsRefusedByANumberSocketLikeAnyTypeMismatch() {
        let source = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSourceNode.typeID)
        let sink = Self.registry.makeNode(NumberSinkNode.typeID)
        let graph = Graph(nodes: [source.id: source, sink.id: sink], links: [])
        #expect(graph.connectionProblem(from: Endpoint(node: source.id, socket: "field"), to: Endpoint(node: sink.id, socket: "value"),
                                        registry: Self.registry) == .typeMismatch(from: .field, to: .number))
    }

    @Test func numbersAreWireableIntoAFieldSocketAndFieldsIntoOne() {
        let number = Self.registry.makeNode(NumberSourceNode.typeID)
        let whole = Self.registry.makeNode(WholeSourceNode.typeID)
        let source = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSourceNode.typeID)
        let sink = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSinkNode.typeID)
        let graph = Graph(nodes: [number.id: number, whole.id: whole, source.id: source, sink.id: sink], links: [])
        for from in [number, whole, source] {
            let socket: SocketName = from.id == source.id ? "field" : "value"
            #expect(graph.connectionProblem(from: Endpoint(node: from.id, socket: socket),
                                            to: Endpoint(node: sink.id, socket: "field"), registry: Self.registry) == nil)
        }
    }

    @Test func aListOfFieldsBroadcastsLikeAnyItemValue() async throws {
        let list = Self.registry.makeNode(NumberListNode.typeID)
        let sink = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSinkNode.typeID)
        let graph = Graph(nodes: [list.id: list, sink.id: sink], links: [wire(list, "values", to: sink)])
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: Self.registry, kernel: FakeKernel()).evaluate(graph, demand: [sink.id])
        let out = try #require(report.results[sink.id]?.outputs?["field"])
        #expect(out.items.map { if case .field(let f) = $0 { f.description } else { "?" } } == ["0", "1", "2"])
    }

    @Test func aWrongTypedConstantInAFieldSocketIsReported() async throws {
        var sink = Self.registry.makeNode(FieldSinkNode.typeID)
        sink.inputValues["field"] = .bool(true)
        let result = try await run([sink], [], demand: sink)
        #expect(result.state == .error("“field” has a value of the wrong type."))
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// Fields spec §2.1: `Field.description` is a one-line recipe, numbers in `Locale.messages`; pinned.
struct FieldDescriptionTests {
    func edges() async throws -> DistanceTarget {
        let solid = try await FakeKernel().extrude(.rectangle(width: 10, height: 10, plane: .xy), distance: 10, mode: .oneSided,
                                                   tag: NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0))
        return .edges(EdgeSet(solid: solid, edges: [EdgeID(0)]))
    }

    @Test func theSpecsExampleReadsAsWritten() async throws {
        let distance = Field.distance(.kernel(try await edges()), signed: false)
        let field = Field.multiply(.remap(distance, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 20, toLow: 1, toHigh: 0, clamped: true),
                                   .noise(seed: 4, scale: 5, octaves: 3))
        #expect(field.description == "remap(distance(edges), 0…20 → 1…0) × noise(seed 4, scale 5, octaves 3)")
    }

    @Test func constantsUseTheMessageLocaleAndNeverPrintMinusZero() {
        #expect(Field.constant(0.5).description == "0.5")
        #expect(Field.constant(1234.5).description == "1,234.5")
        #expect(Field.constant(1.0 / 3).description == "0.33")
        #expect(Field.constant(-2).description == "-2")
        #expect(Field.constant(-0.0).description == "0")
    }

    @Test func theSourcesNameWhatTheyMeasure() async throws {
        let target = try await edges()
        #expect(Field.distance(.point(.zero), signed: false).description == "distance(point)")
        #expect(Field.distance(.profile(.rectangle(width: 1, height: 1, plane: .xy)), signed: false).description == "distance(profile)")
        #expect(Field.distance(.kernel(target), signed: false).description == "distance(edges)")
        guard case .edges(let set) = target else { return }
        #expect(Field.distance(.kernel(.faces(FaceSet(solid: set.solid, faces: [FaceID(0)]))), signed: true).description
            == "distance(faces)")
        #expect(Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(set.solid)), signed: true).description == "distance(solid)")
        #expect(Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(set.solid)), signed: false).description == "distance(solid, unsigned)")
        #expect(Field.linearGradient(plane: .xy, length: 20).description == "linear(length 20)")
        #expect(Field.radialGradient(center: .zero, radius: 7.25).description == "radial(radius 7.25)")
        #expect(Field.noise(seed: -3, scale: 2.5, octaves: 1).description == "noise(seed -3, scale 2.5, octaves 1)")
    }

    @Test func infixMathsBracketsOnlyWhereTheOrderNeedsIt() {
        let one = Field.constant(1), two = Field.constant(2), three = Field.constant(3)
        #expect(Field.add(one, two).description == "1 + 2")
        #expect(Field.multiply(.add(one, two), three).description == "(1 + 2) × 3")
        #expect(Field.add(.multiply(one, two), three).description == "1 × 2 + 3")
        #expect(Field.multiply(one, .multiply(two, three)).description == "1 × 2 × 3")
    }

    @Test func theFunctionsReadLikeCalls() {
        let one = Field.constant(1), two = Field.constant(2)
        #expect(Field.minimum(one, two).description == "min(1, 2)")
        #expect(Field.maximum(one, two).description == "max(1, 2)")
        #expect(Field.clamp(one, low: 0, high: 0.5).description == "clamp(1, 0…0.5)")
        #expect(Field.smoothMinimum(one, two, width: 1.5).description == "smoothMin(1, 2, width 1.5)")
        #expect(Field.remap(one, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 10, toLow: 5, toHigh: -5, clamped: true).description == "remap(1, 0…10 → 5…-5)")
        #expect(Field.remap(one, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 10, toLow: 5, toHigh: -5, clamped: false).description
            == "remap(1, 0…10 → 5…-5, unclamped)")
    }

    @Test func theTooltipFormIsShortenedToEightyCharacters() {
        var field = Field.constant(1)
        for step in 1...30 { field = .add(field, .constant(Double(step))) }
        #expect(field.description.count > 80)
        let short = field.description(limit: 80)
        #expect(short.count == 80 && short.hasSuffix("…") && field.description.hasPrefix(String(short.dropLast())))
        #expect(Field.constant(1).description(limit: 80) == "1")
        #expect(Field.constant(1).description(limit: 0) == "…")
    }
}
```
