### Task 10: The recipe row, the socket tooltip and the editor's wording for fields

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Value+Field.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorEditor/InspectorRow.swift`, `InspectorBuilder.swift`, `InspectorRowView.swift`, `EditorModel+TreeDisplay.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/ValueFieldSummaryTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/FieldDisplayTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Field.description`, `Field.description(limit:)` (Task 3), the built-in field nodes (Tasks 7–9).
- Produces:
  - `Value.fieldSummary(limit: Int? = nil) -> String?`: the recipe of the one field a value holds (cut to `limit` characters when
    given), "3 fields" for several, `nil` for anything else.
  - `InspectorRow.recipe(label: String, text: String)`: a read-only row that puts its text under its label so a long recipe wraps.
  - The inspector page of any node with a field output gains a last section titled "Field" with one `.recipe` row (labelled
    "Field", or the socket's label when the node has several field outputs); before the node has a result the row says
    "No field yet".
  - `EditorModel.socketHelp(of:)` also returns a field's recipe, cut to 80 characters, for the output socket that holds it
    and the input socket it is wired into.

Undo names (fields spec §4) need no new constant: every field node's input follows the existing rule (a fixed label, "Change
From low" for Remap's `fromLow`, "Change B" for Multiply's `b`), and a node is added as "Add Remap". The tests pin that, and
that a number typed into a field socket is stored as a number (the evaluator turns it into a constant field).

Create `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/ValueFieldSummaryTests.swift`:

```swift
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// What a tooltip or the inspector says about the field a value holds.
struct ValueFieldSummaryTests {
    @Test func oneFieldIsItsRecipe() {
        let field = Field.add(.constant(1), .constant(2))
        #expect(Value.one(.field(field)).fieldSummary() == "1 + 2")
        #expect(Value.list([.field(field)]).fieldSummary() == "1 + 2")
    }

    @Test func severalFieldsAreCountedAndAnythingElseHasNoSummary() {
        #expect(Value.list([.field(.constant(1)), .field(.constant(2)), .field(.constant(3))]).fieldSummary() == "3 fields")
        #expect(Value.one(.number(1)).fieldSummary() == nil)
        #expect(Value.list([]).fieldSummary() == nil)
        #expect(Value.list([.field(.constant(1)), .number(2)]).fieldSummary() == nil, "a mixed list is not a list of fields")
    }

    @Test func aLimitCutsTheRecipe() {
        var field = Field.constant(1)
        for step in 1...30 { field = .add(field, .constant(Double(step))) }
        let summary = Value.one(.field(field)).fieldSummary(limit: 80)
        #expect(summary?.count == 80 && summary?.hasSuffix("…") == true)
        #expect(Value.one(.field(field)).fieldSummary() == field.description)
        #expect(Value.list([.field(field), .field(field)]).fieldSummary(limit: 80) == "2 fields")
    }

    @Test func aTreeOfFieldsIsCountedToo() {
        let tree = DataTree.list([.field(.constant(1)), .field(.constant(2))])
        #expect(Value.tree(tree).fieldSummary() == "2 fields")
    }
}
```

Create `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/FieldDisplayTests.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorNodes
import Testing
@testable import CreatorEditor

/// Fields in the editor (fields spec §2.1 and §4): the inspector's "Field" row, the socket tooltip, the undo names and the
/// refusal of a field wired into a number socket.
@MainActor
struct FieldDisplayTests {
    let registry = BuiltInNodes.registry

    func node(_ definition: any NodeDefinition.Type, _ values: [SocketName: ConstantValue] = [:]) -> Node {
        var made = registry.makeNode(definition.typeID)
        made.inputValues.merge(values) { _, given in given }
        made.isOutput = true  // only output nodes are demanded; this one is evaluated
        return made
    }

    func editor(_ nodes: [Node], _ links: [Link] = []) async -> EditorModel {
        let editor = makeEditor(nodes, links, registry: registry)
        await editor.document.waitForEvaluation()
        return editor
    }

    func fieldSection(_ editor: EditorModel, _ node: Node) -> InspectorSectionRows? {
        editor.selection = [node.id]
        return editor.inspectorPage.sections.first { $0.title == "Field" }
    }

    // MARK: the inspector's Field row

    @Test func aNodeWithAFieldOutputShowsItsRecipe() async {
        let radial = node(RadialGradientNode.self, ["radius": .number(7.5)])
        let editor = await editor([radial])
        let expected = InspectorSectionRows(title: "Field", rows: [.recipe(label: "Field", text: "radial(radius 7.5)")])
        #expect(fieldSection(editor, radial) == expected)
    }

    @Test func theRecipeFollowsTheWiringAndIsTheLastSection() async {
        let noise = node(NoiseFieldNode.self, ["seed": .integer(4)])
        let remap = node(FieldRemapNode.self, ["fromLow": .number(0), "fromHigh": .number(20), "toLow": .number(1), "toHigh": .number(0)])
        let editor = await editor([noise, remap], [wire(noise, "field", remap, "field")])
        let section = fieldSection(editor, remap)
        #expect(section?.rows == [.recipe(label: "Field", text: "remap(noise(seed 4, scale 10, octaves 1), 0…20 → 1…0)")])
        #expect(editor.inspectorPage.sections.last?.title == "Field")
    }

    @Test func aNodeThatMakesNoFieldHasNoFieldRow() async {
        let number = node(NumberNode.self)
        let sample = node(SampleFieldNode.self)
        let cull = node(CullByFieldNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([number, sample, cull])
        for other in [number, sample, cull] {
            #expect(fieldSection(editor, other) == nil, "\(other.name)")
        }
    }

    @Test func beforeAResultTheRowSaysSo() {
        let radial = node(RadialGradientNode.self)
        let graph = Graph(nodes: [radial.id: radial], links: [], parameters: [])
        let page = InspectorBuilder.page(graph: graph, selection: [radial.id], registry: registry, results: [:])
        #expect(page.sections.last == InspectorSectionRows(title: "Field", rows: [.recipe(label: "Field", text: "No field yet")]))
    }

    @Test func aListOfFieldsIsCounted() async {
        let series = node(SeriesNode.self, ["count": .integer(3)])
        let add = node(FieldAddNode.self, ["b": .number(10)])
        let editor = await editor([series, add], [wire(series, "values", add, "a")])
        #expect(fieldSection(editor, add)?.rows == [.recipe(label: "Field", text: "3 fields")])
    }

    @Test func theRecipeRowDrawsHeadlessly() async {
        let radial = node(RadialGradientNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([radial])
        editor.selection = [radial.id]
        #expect(!renderHeadless { InspectorPanel(model: editor) }.glyphs.isEmpty)
    }

    // MARK: socket tooltips

    @Test func anOutputAndTheInputItIsWiredIntoShowTheRecipe() async {
        let noise = node(NoiseFieldNode.self, ["seed": .integer(4)])
        let multiply = node(FieldMultiplyNode.self, ["b": .number(0.5)])
        let editor = await editor([noise, multiply], [wire(noise, "field", multiply, "a")])
        #expect(editor.socketHelp(of: noise.id) == ["out.field": "noise(seed 4, scale 10, octaves 1)"])
        let help = editor.socketHelp(of: multiply.id)
        #expect(help["in.a"] == "noise(seed 4, scale 10, octaves 1)")
        #expect(help["in.b"] == nil, "a typed number has no tooltip")
        #expect(help["out.field"] == "noise(seed 4, scale 10, octaves 1) × 0.5")
    }

    @Test func aLongRecipeIsCutToEightyCharacters() async {
        var nodes: [Node] = [node(NoiseFieldNode.self)]
        var links: [Link] = []
        for _ in 0..<6 {
            let add = node(FieldAddNode.self, ["b": .number(12345.5)])
            links.append(wire(nodes[nodes.count - 1], "field", add, "a"))
            nodes.append(add)
        }
        let editor = await editor(nodes, links)
        let help = editor.socketHelp(of: nodes[nodes.count - 1].id)["out.field"]
        #expect(help?.count == 80 && help?.hasSuffix("…") == true, "\(String(describing: help))")
    }

    @Test func numbersAndSamplesHaveNoFieldTooltip() async {
        let number = node(NumberNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([number])
        #expect(editor.socketHelp(of: number.id).isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func aListOfFieldsShowsItsCount() async {
        let series = node(SeriesNode.self, ["count": .integer(3)])
        let add = node(FieldAddNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([series, add], [wire(series, "values", add, "a")])
        #expect(editor.socketHelp(of: add.id)["out.field"] == "3 fields")
    }

    // MARK: undo names and typed numbers

    @Test func addingFieldNodesNamesTheStepAfterTheType() {
        let editor = makeEditor([], registry: registry)
        _ = editor.addFromLibrary(FieldRemapNode.typeID)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Add Remap")
        _ = editor.addFromLibrary(DistanceFieldNode.typeID)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Add Distance Field")
    }

    /// Decision 9: the spec's names, so adding these two reads "Undo Add Add" and "Undo Add Multiply". Pinned here so that choosing
    /// "Add Fields" and "Multiply Fields" instead is a visible change to these lines and to the node's display names.
    @Test func addingAddOrMultiplyReadsOddlyAndIsPinned() {
        let editor = makeEditor([], registry: registry)
        _ = editor.addFromLibrary(FieldAddNode.typeID)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Add Add")
        _ = editor.addFromLibrary(FieldMultiplyNode.typeID)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Add Multiply")
    }

    @Test func aNumberTypedIntoAFieldSocketIsStoredAsANumberAndNamedByItsLabel() async throws {
        let multiply = node(FieldMultiplyNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([multiply])
        editor.selection = [multiply.id]
        let rows = try #require(editor.inspectorPage.sections.first?.rows)
        guard case .number(let b) = rows[1] else {
            Issue.record("expected a number row for b")
            return
        }
        editor.setNumber(b, to: 0.5)
        #expect(editor.graph.nodes[multiply.id]?.inputValues["b"] == .number(0.5))
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Change B")
        await editor.document.waitForEvaluation()
        editor.selection = [multiply.id]
        #expect(editor.inspectorPage.sections.last?.rows == [.recipe(label: "Field", text: "1 × 0.5")])
    }

    @Test func remapsInputsAreNamedByTheirLabels() async throws {
        let remap = node(FieldRemapNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([remap])
        editor.selection = [remap.id]
        let rows = editor.inspectorPage.sections.first?.rows ?? []
        guard case .number(let fromLow)? = rows.first else {
            Issue.record("expected a number row")
            return
        }
        editor.setNumber(fromLow, to: 2)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Change From low")
    }

    @Test func cullByFieldsSegmentedInputsAreNamedByTheirLabels() async throws {
        let cull = node(CullByFieldNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([cull])
        editor.selection = [cull.id]
        let sections = editor.inspectorPage.sections
        guard case .segmented(let mode, _, _)? = sections.first(where: { $0.title == "Cull" })?.rows.first,
              case .segmented(let keep, _, _)? = sections.first(where: { $0.title == "Threshold" })?.rows.last else {
            Issue.record("expected segmented rows for mode and keep")
            return
        }
        editor.setInput(mode, to: .integer(1))
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Change Mode")
        editor.setInput(keep, to: .integer(1))
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Change Keep")
    }

    // MARK: refusing a field where a number goes

    @Test func aFieldCannotBeWiredIntoANumberInput() async {
        let radial = node(RadialGradientNode.self)
        let circle = node(CircleNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([radial, circle])
        editor.connect(wire(radial, "field", circle, "diameter"))
        #expect(editor.graph.links.isEmpty)
        #expect(editor.refusal?.message == "A field can't connect to a number input.")
    }

    @Test func aNumberCanBeWiredIntoAFieldInputInOneNamedStep() async {
        let number = node(NumberNode.self)
        let add = node(FieldAddNode.self)
        let editor = await editor([number, add])
        editor.connect(wire(number, "value", add, "a"))
        #expect(editor.graph.links.count == 1 && editor.refusal == nil)
        #expect(editor.document.undoName == "Connect")
    }
}
```

```swift
extension Value {
    /// What a tooltip or the inspector says about the field this value holds (fields spec §2.1): the recipe of one field, cut to
    /// `limit` characters when a limit is given, or "3 fields" for several. `nil` when the value holds anything else, or nothing.
    public func fieldSummary(limit: Int? = nil) -> String? {
        let fields = items.compactMap { scalar -> Field? in if case .field(let field) = scalar { field } else { nil } }
        guard !fields.isEmpty, fields.count == items.count else { return nil }
        guard fields.count == 1, let only = fields.first else { return "\(fields.count) fields" }
        return limit.map { only.description(limit: $0) } ?? only.description
    }
}
```

            sections.append(data)

    /// The "Field" section (fields spec §2.1): the recipe of the field each field output of the node holds, read-only. `nil` for a
    /// node with no field output. Its row is labelled "Field", or by the socket when there are several; before the node has a
    /// result it says "No field yet".
    static func fieldSection(node: Node, registry: NodeRegistry, results: [NodeID: NodeResult]) -> InspectorSectionRows? {
        let outputs = registry.outputs(for: node).filter { $0.type == .field }
        guard !outputs.isEmpty else { return nil }
        let rows = outputs.map { spec -> InspectorRow in
            let text = results[node.id]?.outputs?[spec.name]?.fieldSummary() ?? "No field yet"
            return .recipe(label: outputs.count == 1 ? "Field" : InspectorLabel.text(for: spec.name), text: text)
        }
        return InspectorSectionRows(title: "Field", rows: rows)
    }

    /// The tooltip of each socket of `node` that carries a data tree, keyed like `NodeRowModel.id` ("in.tree",
    /// "out.values"): "Tree 3 × 8". An input shows the tree wired into it. A socket that carries a field shows its recipe, cut

    /// Sockets carrying one item or a flat list of anything else have no entry, and neither does a node with no result yet.
    public func socketHelp(of node: NodeID) -> [String: String] {
        var help: [String: String] = [:]
        for link in graph.links where link.to.node == node {
            if let tree = tree(on: link.from) {
                help["in.\(link.to.socket.rawValue)"] = "Tree \(tree.shapeText)"
            } else if let recipe = result(of: link.from.node)?.outputs?[link.from.socket]?.fieldSummary(limit: Self.tooltipLimit) {
                help["in.\(link.to.socket.rawValue)"] = recipe
            }

            if case .tree(let tree) = value {
                help["out.\(socket.rawValue)"] = "Tree \(tree.shapeText)"
            } else if let recipe = value.fieldSummary(limit: Self.tooltipLimit) {
                help["out.\(socket.rawValue)"] = recipe
            }
        }
        return help
    }

### Task 11: `FieldBench` rows 1 to 3 and the performance record

**Files:**
- Create: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBenchFixtures.swift`, `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBench.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBenchFixturesTests.swift`
- Modify: `docs/verification/performance.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `FieldSampler`, `FieldDistanceCache`, `SampleFieldNode`, `CullByFieldNode`, `OCCTKernel` (Tasks 2 to 9), `Bench`, `BenchSamples` (exist).
- Produces: `FieldBench` (run by `scripts/bench.sh FieldBench`; skipped by a plain `swift test`) and `FieldBenchFixtures`, which the
  fixture tests check and which 7b-3 extends with Field Scatter's row.

The fields spec §7 lists four rows and §9 gives this plan "the first three". Row 3 is *Field Scatter, 2,000 points on a face
< 50 ms*, and Field Scatter is 7b-3's. So row 3 here measures the work Field Scatter starts with, at the same size and
against the same 50 ms: Sample Field and then Cull by Field over a tree of 2,000 placements. 7b-3 adds the real Field Scatter
row as `field-3b` and row 4 (the edit to the tinted preview) beside these.

Row 2's budget (under 100 ms for 10,000 signed distances the first time, and about nothing cached) is the one the design
risks: `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` costs tens of microseconds a point, so `occt_distances` (Task 2) shares the points out in
blocks across OCCT's thread pool and skips the classifier for a point outside the solid's bounds. A miss on an idle Mac is
recorded with its numbers, never hidden (parent spec §7.3).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBenchFixtures.swift`:

```swift
// Test fixture file: the fields and points the field benchmarks sample (fields spec §7).
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// What `FieldBench` measures, built here so a plain `swift test` can check it is what the benchmark says it is.
enum FieldBenchFixtures {
    /// Row 1: a source, a remap, noise, a multiply and a clamp (five nodes, no kernel distance).
    static let fiveNodeField: Field = {
        let radial = Field.radialGradient(center: Vector3(5, -3, 2), radius: 40)
        let remapped = Field.remap(radial, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 1, toLow: 1, toHigh: 0, clamped: true)
        let noise = Field.noise(seed: 4, scale: 8, octaves: 2)
        return .clamp(.multiply(remapped, noise), low: 0, high: 1)
    }()

    /// `count` points spread over a 100 × 80 × 20 region, the same every time.
    static func points(_ count: Int) -> [Vector3] {
        (0..<count).map { index in
            Vector3(Double(index % 101) - 50, Double((index * 7) % 81) - 40, Double((index * 3) % 21) - 5)
        }
    }

    /// Row 2: the plate whose signed distance is sampled, 60 × 40 × 6 with R4 corners.
    static func plate(_ kernel: any Kernel) async throws -> Solid {
        try await kernel.extrude(.roundedRectangle(width: 60, height: 40, radius: 4, plane: .xy), distance: 6, mode: .oneSided,
                                 tag: NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0))
    }

    /// Row 2b: a smooth loft through three circles (Ø10, Ø6 and Ø12 at 0, 10 and 20 mm), whose faces are B-spline, not planes and
    /// cylinders: the cost of a point against a solid the 100 ms target was never measured on.
    static func loft(_ kernel: any Kernel) async throws -> Solid {
        let sections = [(0.0, 5.0), (10, 3), (20, 6)].map { height, radius in
            Profile2D.circle(radius: radius, center: .zero, plane: Plane.xy.offset(by: height))
        }
        return try await kernel.loft(sections, ruled: false, tag: NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0))
    }

    /// Row 3: `rows` branches of `columns` placements 4 mm apart, as a Grid Pattern makes them.
    static func placements(rows: Int, columns: Int) -> DataTree {
        let branches = (0..<rows).map { row in
            DataTree.list((0..<columns).map { column in .plane(.through(Vector3(Double(column) * 4, Double(row) * 4, 0))) })
        }
        return DataTree(depth: 2, branches: branches) ?? .empty(depth: 2)
    }

    /// Row 3's field: noise plus a radial gradient over the placements' region.
    static let placementField = Field.add(.noise(seed: 9, scale: 12, octaves: 2), .radialGradient(center: Vector3(100, 80, 0), radius: 160))
}
```

Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBench.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import CreatorOCCT
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// Fields spec §7, rows 1 to 3 (row 4, the tinted preview, comes with the previews in 7b-3). Release, idle Mac; prints
/// `BENCH field-…` lines, with each row's budget judged on the median:
/// 1. a five-node field (a source, a remap, noise, a multiply, a clamp) over 10,000 points, no kernel distance: under 5 ms;
/// 2. the signed distance from a plate to 10,000 points on OCCT: under 100 ms the first time, about nothing cached (a plate is
///    planes and cylinders; 2b is the same points against a smooth loft, recorded and not gated);
/// 3. the fields spec puts Field Scatter's 2,000 points on a face here; Field Scatter arrives in 7b-3, so this row measures the
///    work it will start with at the same size and budget, Sample Field and then Cull by Field over a tree of 2,000
///    placements (50 ms). 7b-3 adds the Field Scatter row beside it.
@MainActor
@Suite(.serialized, .enabled(if: Bench.isRequested, "set METALCREATOR_BENCH=1 (scripts/bench.sh)"))
struct FieldBench {
    static let fiveNodeBudget = 5.0
    static let distanceBudget = 100.0
    static let placementsBudget = 50.0

    @Test func aFiveNodeFieldOverTenThousandPoints() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let points = FieldBenchFixtures.points(10_000)
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("field-1 five nodes × 10,000 points (no kernel)")
        for round in 0..<65 {
            let start = clock.now
            let values = try await FieldSampler.sample(FieldBenchFixtures.fiveNodeField, at: points, kernel: kernel)
            guard round >= 5 else { continue }
            samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
            #expect(values.count == points.count)
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.fiveNodeBudget))
    }

    @Test func theSignedDistanceToASolidOverTenThousandPoints() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let plate = try await FieldBenchFixtures.plate(kernel)
        let field = Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(plate)), signed: true)
        let points = FieldBenchFixtures.points(10_000)
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var first = BenchSamples("field-2 signed distance to a plate × 10,000 points, first (OCCT)")
        var cached = BenchSamples("field-2 signed distance to a plate × 10,000 points, cached")
        for round in 0..<25 {
            let cache = FieldDistanceCache()  // a new cache each round, so the first sampling always asks the kernel
            let begin = clock.now
            let values = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: points, kernel: kernel, cache: cache)
            let middle = clock.now
            _ = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: points, kernel: kernel, cache: cache)
            let end = clock.now
            guard round >= 3 else { continue }
            first.append(from: begin, to: middle)
            cached.append(from: middle, to: end)
            #expect(values.count == points.count)
        }
        print(first.line(budget: Self.distanceBudget))
        print(cached.line())
    }

    /// Recorded, not gated (decision 13): the same 10,000 points against a smooth loft, which has B-spline faces.
    @Test func theSignedDistanceToALoftOverTenThousandPoints() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let loft = try await FieldBenchFixtures.loft(kernel)
        let field = Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(loft)), signed: true)
        let points = FieldBenchFixtures.points(10_000)
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var first = BenchSamples("field-2b signed distance to a smooth loft × 10,000 points, first (OCCT)")
        for round in 0..<10 {
            let begin = clock.now
            let values = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: points, kernel: kernel, cache: FieldDistanceCache())
            guard round >= 2 else { continue }
            first.append(from: begin, to: clock.now)
            #expect(values.count == points.count)
        }
        print(first.line())
    }

    @Test func sampleFieldAndCullByFieldOverTwoThousandPlacements() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let tree = FieldBenchFixtures.placements(rows: 40, columns: 50)
        let registry = BuiltInNodes.registry
        let sampleContext = EvalContext(node: registry.makeNode(SampleFieldNode.typeID), item: 0, parameters: [:])
        let cullContext = EvalContext(node: registry.makeNode(CullByFieldNode.typeID), item: 0, parameters: [:])
        let field = Scalar.field(FieldBenchFixtures.placementField)
        let sampleInputs = NodeInputs(item: 0, slots: ["field": .item(field), "at": .tree(tree)])
        let cullInputs = NodeInputs(item: 0, slots: [
            "placements": .tree(tree), "field": .item(field), "mode": .item(.integer(1)), "level": .item(.number(0.5)),
            "keep": .item(.integer(0)), "seed": .item(.integer(3)),
        ])
        let kernel = FakeKernel()
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("field-3 Sample Field + Cull by Field × 2,000 placements")
        for round in 0..<45 {
            let start = clock.now
            let sampled = try await SampleFieldNode.evaluate(sampleInputs, kernel: kernel, context: sampleContext)
            let culled = try await CullByFieldNode.evaluate(cullInputs, kernel: kernel, context: cullContext)
            guard round >= 5 else { continue }
            samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
            #expect(sampled.trees["values"]?.itemCount == 2000 && culled.trees["placements"] != nil)
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.placementsBudget))
    }
}
```

```
## Raw output
```

with:

```
## Fields (fields spec §7, sub-project 7b-1)

**Targets**, release build on an idle Mac, the median of each row judged against its budget (`FieldBench.fiveNodeBudget`,
`distanceBudget`, `placementsBudget`). A miss is recorded with its numbers, never hidden (parent spec §7.3).

| Row | Case | Target |
|---|---|---|
| `field-1` | a five-node field (a radial gradient, a remap, noise, a multiply, a clamp), no kernel distance, 10,000 points | under **5 ms** |
| `field-2` | the signed distance from a 60 × 40 × 6 plate (R4 corners) to 10,000 points on OCCT, the first time and cached | first under **100 ms** (a gate: see below), cached about nothing |
| `field-2b` | the same points against a smooth loft through three circles (B-spline faces), first | recorded, no budget |
| `field-3` | Sample Field then Cull by Field (Probability) over 2,000 placements in 40 branches; stands in for Field Scatter's row (7b-3) at its size and budget | under **50 ms** |
| `field-4` | the edit to a tinted preview on the bracket | 7b-3 |

**The 100 ms is for analytic solids.** The plate is planes and cylinders. A point against a smooth loft's B-spline faces cost about
0.9 ms on a saturated machine (3,000 points took 2.7 s at a load of about 168), roughly forty times the plate's, so 10,000 points
against such a solid will not meet 100 ms on any machine this plan knows of, and `field-2b` records what it does. Nobody should read
the plate's number as the cost of every solid.

**What is measured** (`Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/FieldBench.swift`, fixtures `FieldBenchFixtures`): row 1 and 3 call
`FieldSampler.sample` and the two nodes' `evaluate` directly on the Fake kernel; row 2 samples a plate extruded by `OCCTKernel`
with a new `FieldDistanceCache` each round (so the first sampling always asks the kernel) and then again with the same cache
(the cached time is the cache's lookup: hashing 10,000 points and comparing them).

**How row 2 is kept fast.** `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` costs about 28 µs a point on this plate (CPU time on one thread,
measured with the fixture's plate and points in a standalone program), and a solid classification about 18 µs more. So
`occt_distances` asks the classifier only about points inside the solid's bounds (9% of the fixture's points) and shares the
points out in blocks of 64 over OCCT's thread pool: one thread is about 280 ms for 10,000 points, so the 100 ms target needs
about four cores working. The threads change no number: serial and threaded results were identical in the standalone program
and `aBatchGivesWhatEachPointGivesOnItsOwn` pins batch equals single on OCCT.

**Fixture checks** run in a plain `swift test` (`FieldBenchFixturesTests`): row 1's field has five nodes and gives varied
numbers in 0…1, the points are the same every time, row 2's plate and row 2b's loft give the right signed distances on OCCT, row
3's tree is 2,000 placements in 40 branches.

**Results: not an idle run.** Taken while verifying the plan, on a MacBookPro18,2 (10 cores, macOS 27.0.1) that other builds had saturated (load averages 120.51
160.05 156.74 before, 136.45 162.56 160.51 after), so every number here is an upper bound and none is a verdict:

```text
BENCH field-1 five nodes × 10,000 points (no kernel): median 0.86 ms, p95 1.32 ms, max 2.31 ms (n 60); budget 5.00 ms: met
BENCH field-2 signed distance to a plate × 10,000 points, first (OCCT): median 850.80 ms, p95 1081.93 ms, max 1133.94 ms (n 22); budget 100.00 ms: missed
BENCH field-2 signed distance to a plate × 10,000 points, cached: median 4.18 ms, p95 74.11 ms, max 114.87 ms (n 22)
BENCH field-3 Sample Field + Cull by Field × 2,000 placements: median 0.43 ms, p95 1.29 ms, max 1.57 ms (n 40); budget 50.00 ms: met
```

Rows 1 and 3 met their budgets even so. Row 2's first-time distance did not: 851 ms against 100 ms. Before the shim shared the points
out over threads, the same fixture measured 3,545 ms under a similar load, so the threads help, but a saturated machine gives them
no cores to run on. **Row 2 is unverified, and it is a gate:** Task 11 does not end, and the branch is not merged, until an idle
run (all three load averages under 3) puts row 2's median under 100 ms and its lines replace these. If it misses, pull the levers
in this order, re-running after each: (1) a fast path for planar faces in the shim (a point-to-plane distance and a point-in-face
test in place of `Extrema_ExtPS`; `DistanceConformanceTests` must still pass unchanged), (2) a coarser classifier tolerance, and
then stop and ask the user (decision 13): the budget is not edited and a miss is not called met. Rows 1 and 3, idle, are expected
well under their budgets; `field-2b` is recorded whatever it is.

## Raw output
```

  `*_free`; no C++ exception crosses into Swift. `occt_distances` (`cocct_distance.cpp`) measures points to a solid's faces,
  picked faces or picked edges with `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` (to the faces, not the solid: OCCT answers 0 for a point
  inside a solid) and signs a solid's distance with `BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier`, only for a point inside the solid's bounds; it
  shares the points out in blocks of 64 over OCCT's thread pool while `OCCTKernel.serialized` is held, and the caller's buffer
  is the output, so it allocates nothing to free. `OCCTKernel` calls it 1,024 points at a time, checking for cancellation between.
- `CreatorGraph`: graph model, sockets, broadcasting, `Evaluator` (cached, cancellable), commands and undo,
```

with:

```
    anywhere before it. So a pick on one instance survives a cull of another and a pick on a culled one warns naming its path.
  - Fields (`Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields`, category `NodeCategory.fields`, library section "Fields", after Lists & Trees; fields spec
    §3): `FieldSampler.sample(_:at:kernel:cache:)` is the one way to read a field: it walks the expression once per batch over
    structure-of-arrays `[Double]`s, checks cancellation every 1,024 points, asks the kernel once per distance leaf (through the
    evaluator's `FieldDistanceCache` when given), and never lets a value depend on the rest of the batch; its one `switch` over
    `Field` has no `default`, so a new case (Image Field, 7b-2) is a compile error there, in `Field.children` and in
    `Field.description`. `FieldNoise` is 3D simplex noise hashed with `SeededRandom` (purpose `.fieldNoise`, the octave as the stream;
    `.fieldCull` is Cull by Field's; both pinned in `FieldNoiseTests` like the others). The sources are Distance Field (`target` is
    an `any` socket: a vector, edge set, profile, face set or solid; `mode` Unsigned / Signed matters to a solid only), Linear
    Gradient, Radial Gradient and Noise Field; the maths nodes (`Maths/`) are Add, Multiply, Min / Max, Remap, Clamp and Smooth
    Blend, each over `.field` item sockets, so a list of fields broadcasts and a number typed or wired in is a constant field.
    Sample Field (`at` is a `plane` socket with `tree` access, so points and placements both wire) returns numbers in the shape of
    `at`, culled gaps and paths included, from one sampler call; a single plane gives a list of one. Cull by Field keeps placements
    by Threshold (Above and Below both keep a value exactly on the level) or Probability (a `.fieldCull` draw at the item's kept
    index, stream = branch ordinal, below the field held to 0…1, as Random Reduce draws), keeping paths; both refuse a list in any
    input in the layouts' words (`singleValuesRefusal` = `LayoutChecks.singleValues`). The graded-sizes chain is a field → Remap →
    Sample Field → a shortcut's size socket (Remap takes and returns a field, so the spec's reverse order cannot be wired; fields
    spec Errata (7b-1)). Fields are computed, never saved, and a group interface refuses a `field` socket
    (`GroupRefusal.fieldSocket`, in `GraphContent.checkInterface`): `"field"` in a format-6 file would not decode in an older
    build, so 7b-2's format 7 lifts it. The distance cache counts the solids its entries keep alive. `FieldBench`
    (`Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench`): rows 1 and 2 are the spec's (row 2's 100 ms is for analytic solids and gates the merge;
    `field-2b` records a smooth loft); row 3 measures Sample Field and Cull by Field over 2,000 placements until Field Scatter
    (7b-3) can be measured at the same size.
- `CreatorStyle`: colour themes (spec §6.6, Dracula by default), the only place colour hex values are written.

- [ ] **Step 3b: Record the errata in the fields spec**

Append to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-fields-design.md`:

```

## Errata (7b-1: the fields core)

- **Graded sizes: Remap comes before Sample Field (§3.3, §8).** Remap takes and returns a field and there is no numeric Remap, so
  "Sample Field → Remap → a shortcut's size socket" cannot be wired (a field into a number socket is refused, field → number).
  The chain is a field → **Remap → Sample Field** → the size socket. §8's acceptance test (Sample Field → Remap → Hole Pattern
  sizes) is built in that order; `GradedHolesTests` (7b-1) already does it on OCCT.
- **A list of fields into Sample Field or Cull by Field is refused (§2.1).** The maths nodes broadcast a list or tree of fields;
  these two work on a whole tree through a `tree`-access socket, which cannot also broadcast, so any list in any of their inputs
  is refused in the layouts' words ("… takes single values, not lists: …"). A field per branch is a later change to both nodes.
- **A group's interface cannot carry a field under format 6 (D4, §5).** `"field"` in a format-6 file would not decode in an older
  build (CLAUDE.md: that bumps the format), so `GraphContent` refuses a `.field` interface socket; 7b-2's format 7 lifts it. A
  field chain wholly inside a group works.
- **The 100 ms of §7 row 2 is for analytic solids (planes, cylinders).** A smooth loft's B-spline faces cost about forty times a
  plate's per point; `field-2b` records it without a budget.
- **The distance cache (§2.2) counts the solids it keeps alive** (`Solid.estimatedBytes`) with its points and distances, so its
  64 MB bounds what it pins.
```

**How this plan was verified.** Every code block was applied, by a script that writes each `Create` block and makes each `Edit`
replace its quoted text exactly once (a block whose old text does not match once is an error), to a scratch copy of `master`
`8cf30f2` beside a MetalUI copy. It was run twice: the first draft task by task (`swift build --build-tests`, a full `swift test`
by the rule in the Global Constraints, `swiftlint lint --strict`, each task's tests first failing to compile or run); the revision
(this one) as a whole, with these results. Against MetalUI `930db4a`: build with no new warning (only
`ContextMenuTests.swift:153`), the full run exit 0, 11 "Test run with" lines summing to 3,401, no "recorded an issue" or "failed
after"; `swiftlint lint --strict` zero violations. Against MetalUI `ff8a1ea4` (copied with `git archive`, read-only): exit 1, 3,401
tests, 11 "Test run with" lines, and the Gate 0 baseline exactly (two "recorded an issue" lines and three "failed after" lines, all
`aPrimaryPressOnTheSameElementLeavesTheLostDragToTheNextDragValues`, the same two issues that pristine `master` gives against
`ff8a1ea4`). The new tests were each shown to fail without their code: with every `Task.checkCancellation()` removed from
`FieldSampler+Maths.swift` `everyLoopStopsBetweenChunksWhenCancelled` and `aSamplingCancelledInsideTheKernelStopsAtTheNextLoop`
fail (before, all 23 `FieldSamplerTests` passed); with the loop's check removed from `OCCTKernel.distances`
`aBatchCancelledBetweenChunksStopsThere` fails; without `target.solid.estimatedBytes` in `Entry.bytes` three cache tests fail;
without the interface check two `GroupFieldTests` fail. Not run: `scripts/bench.sh FieldBench` (the machine was saturated, load
150 to 280, so no number would be a verdict); Task 11's gate stands.

**Not verified by the plan, and said so.** Row 2 on an idle Mac, and `field-2b` on any Mac. The OCCT thread-safety of
`occt_distances` on B-spline faces is covered by one loft (`aBatchOnALoftGivesWhatEachPointGivesOnItsOwn`, run on a saturated
machine), not by an exhaustive set of surface types.

**A fact about the environment, not the plan.** MetalUI's `master` moved to `ff8a1ea4` (PR #72, MetalUI C26, the fix for gap VI-b: "A press
that continues a gesture arena fails a drag that had reported, without `onCancelled`"). Against it
`ViewportCancelWindowTests.aPrimaryPressOnTheSameElementLeavesTheLostDragToTheNextDragValues` records two issues, because it
pins the old (buggy) behaviour; pristine `master` fails it too, so the fields work does not cause it, and it belongs to the track
that adopts C26. The first draft said to build against `930db4a`; that cannot be done without editing `../MetalUI` or
`Package.swift`'s path, both forbidden, so the pass rule and Gate 0 (Global Constraints) judge against `ff8a1ea4` instead, and
decision 12 offers the user the other two ways out.

**Execution.** Do the tasks in order; each ends in a commit and leaves the package green. Tasks 1 to 6 are independent of the
editor and could be reviewed together; 7 to 9 need 3 to 6; 10 needs 7 to 9. Plan complete and saved to
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-7b-core.md`. Please review the plan and the ten decisions near its top. Subagent-driven
execution is recommended: the tasks share interfaces (Tasks 3, 4, 6 and 9 are used by many later ones) and a fresh reviewer per task
catches an interface drift before it spreads.
